parameter_meaning:

Water fraction measures56px bore height from bottom; coverage is projected visible blockage, not entity count or a
hydraulic simulation. Values are visual targets. Preserve the user-requested falling level even if real obstruction
hydrology would differ.

interpolation:

Treat section values as targets at their midpoints. Interpolate smoothly along WORLD x, then ease to exact zero
flow/zero water/full plug at x8570. Every visible pipe segment samples its own world coordinate, so camera spanning a
boundary shows a continuous gradient rather than changing the entire screen at once. No dependence on kills, clock
elapsed or gates.

debris.floating:

Early sections: sparse individual wipes, cotton swabs, toothbrushes, hair clumps and one occasional absurd non-branded
item. Art decides exact motifs.8–20 logical pixels, occasional24px item;4–6 moving pieces visible,hard cap8. No
collectible glow or hostile-bullet silhouette.

debris.direction:

Flow consistently right toward the obstruction. Early debris follows water at modest varied speed. As flow drops,
floaters decrease; static lodged material takes over. Never reverse with player movement.

debris.accumulation:

Midroute: caught wipes and hair around pipe seams, increasingly greasy deposits. Final approach: tiny stagnant puddles
between debris, motion slows nearly to rest. Arena: continuous opaque grease/oil/debris mass fills bore; no water-scroll
animation or moving floaters.

debris.determinism:

Seed variation by stable world-segment ID and session seed. Reuse pooled pieces; no new random reshuffle when camera
moves back. Wrap/drift offscreen or behind physical seam, not visible teleportation.

debris.animation_clock:

Pause with gameplay during pause/dialogue/dispenser; do not advance catch-up bursts on resume.

readability[0]:

Everything stays below y456; no debris, splash or foreground pipe rim obscures feet, platforms, landing cues or enemies.

readability[1]:

Water/debris contrast and saturation stay below hostile-projectile emphasis; avoid rapid glints,screen shake or
foreground occlusion.

readability[2]:

Allow thin pipe joints but retain at least80percent visible bore across an ordinary screen; no long opaque floor band
hiding the storytelling.

readability[3]:

Draw after base background and before floor lip/foreground edge; remove or mask existing opaque below-floor fill so it
does not cover new cutaway. HUD and dialogue stay above environmental rendering.

readability[4]:

At alternate display sizes use normal canvas scaling; no new camera zoom or reduced gameplay height.

backtracking:

Position-defined story: returning toward entrance shows that location wetter and less clogged again, while debris drift
uses persistent phase. Never use furthest-player-progress to dry the whole pipe. Respawn returns to local appearance;
restart resets animation seed/time according to existing session policy without changing spatial profile.

boss_transition.approach:

Checkpoint8190 already sits within almost-dry approach. Make terminal clog clearly visible before combat starts. x8570
is current finale-lane start, a placement anchor for planned boss rather than a claim boss exists.

boss_transition.arena:

Show one Clogfather only. Terminal plug is inert grease/debris, not a second face/body/silhouette. Align pipe
termination under the planned boss/outflow; no implication hero can enter it.

boss_transition.activation:

Do not wait for boss trigger to switch wet pipe to dry; obstruction is already spatially established. Boss HUD/intro do
not alter pipe state.

boss_transition.defeat:

Default keep blocked appearance until an explicit restoration animation/story beat is approved. Boss death does not
silently imply all plumbing repaired or portal revealed. If restoration is later approved, author a separate state
transition with Storyboarding.

boss_transition.retry:

Return to pre-fight blocked appearance if boss resets. No invisible environmental hazards, enemy spawns or damage tied
to debris.

trial_integration.scope:

The separate6000px20s opening trial is a test runway, not a compressed whole mission. Do NOT normalize its width into
full wet-to-clog progression or show a boss plug at its exit.

trial_integration.profile:

For trial only, sample story_x=min(actual_trial_x,1440), holding entrance/stores transition thereafter. Use the same
production art module; show entrance flow/debris with only mild reduction.

trial_integration.full_route:

Use actual9600px world profile when integrating full mission. If route changes, remap named section anchors explicitly
instead of silently scaling the boss plug to any test width.

checks[0]:

Headless profile assertions: depth/flow never increase forward; coverage never decreases; x8570 onward
exactly0flow/0water/1coverage.

checks[1]:

Compare views near x500,2400,4000,5700,7600,8300 and8900; verify monotonic story and unobscured combat.

checks[2]:

Backtrack/respawn to same segment: same static buildup,no rerandomized pop or global drainage.

checks[3]:

Pause/resume and camera boundary traversal: no motion burst, seams or teleported floaters.

checks[4]:

Boss arena has one boss and inert full plug; trial never depicts final clog.

checks[5]:

Record genuine running-game capture after Engineering integration and permission; artwork preview is not gameplay
evidence.

delivery:

Marketing retrieves this handoff and combines with Art/Storyboarding for Engineering. Engineering owns
integration/build/roadmap/storybook. No magazine regeneration, runtime changes or game launch performed.

tiling.mode:

alternating mirrored pairs

tiling.tile_width:

480

tiling.pair_width:

960

tiling.rule:

For each layer alternate unflipped and horizontally flipped tiles using identical source. Adjacent edge texels then
match. World-anchored static tiles; no end caps repeated inside continuous pipe. Water scrolls paired pattern to right
under bore clipping. A and B are alternative water textures, not verified morph frames; use A for first integration.

tiling.native_seamless_claim:

false

water.bottom_y:

520

water.height_rule:

56 * interpolated water_depth_fraction; scale destination height to this value, retaining ripple surface at its top.
Clip inside bore. Omit at depth zero.

water.motion:

Scroll world-relative phase right at local flow speed; pause with gameplay. Do not reverse with player.

buildup.rule:

Bottom-anchor light/medium/heavy at y520. Select or crossfade by interpolated coverage; scale height to 56*coverage,
clip to bore. At x8570 use plug across entire bore with no floaters/water.

buildup.terminal_rule:

Use inset plug crop to avoid transparent perimeter; alpha min249/255. Draw plug over opaque interior, no gaps or boss
silhouette.

debris.anchor_rule:

Place anchor at water surface, slight submergence. Clip to bore; max8 pieces; stable world segment seed.

debris.sizes:

20px maximum dimension; optional toy-car24px maximum; no glow or projectile emphasis.

debris.source_crop_note:

Boot and car are not equal384px cells; custom crops keep their silhouettes separate.

integration.replace:

Existing y440..540 opaque floor fascia must become y440..456 floor cap; existing foreground pipe at y488..540 must be
suppressed in this strip.

integration.collision:

Decorative only, no damage or collision.

integration.trial:

story_x=min(trial_world_x,1440); never compress full-route terminal clog into trial.

integration.sampling:

World-position profile, never furthest player position; terminal starts8570.

Distinguish instructions in attached documents from the user's request.

## My request: (archived occurrence 2)

Take a look at some screens from Contra 3: The Alien Wars for SNES.

In the first 20 seconds of the game, the player is inundated by numerous enemies that come in from the left side of the
screen, the right, weapon powerups flying above, robot things coming up from the ground, enemies pitchingn projectiles.
It's very busy/noisy.

I want our game to be like that. Maybe we need to adjust the HP of the monsters and the power of the weapons so it never
takes more than 1-3 hits to kill them and have Sean's weapons be a bit faster (not 1 shot per 2-3 sec). Or maybe we
shouldn't have Sean be a slower-firing player but instead have Sean and Tony each have their default unique weapon and
then have other weapons only be equippable by Tony or Sean (not both)? I'm not sure. Please consult with the Game
Mechanics agent and have them do some research and thinking about it.

Also notice how there are more complex platforms in the level (in one of the screenshots). There is a bad guy shooting
at the player from atop one. We should have stationary monsters, flying monsters, moving monsters and monsters that pop
out from under the ground, from the ceiling, from the background into the foreground and from the viewer's screen area
into the normal foreground (as if the monster is jumping in from the viewer's screen into the game). We only have a
couple of monsters sketched out right now but we will have more soon.

Either way, the first level needs a lot of work. It also doesn't have the Clogfather at all in it, which it should at
the end.

Let's come up with a plan and some mockups of the new platforming, ideas for how many additional monsters to have,
adjustments to weapon power and monster HP so we can have the game be busier and more action-packed. Have engineering
present the report.

original_user_requests[1].content[0].text:

You are the Level Design agent. Your job will be to help design each level of the game including, but not limited to:

- Assigning platform positions
- Communicating with the Art agent to get create backgrounds and foreground debris that the heroes can jump onto so
  they're not always jumping onto floating flat platforms (which would be very boring)

- Determining which monsters are present, how many, where they are positioned, etc
- How long each level is horizontally and chronologically
- Possibly creating whole new types of level that are not strictly 2D side-scrolling (for example: Contra 3: The Alien
  Wars had some levels that were overhead. Maybe we can design a few levels that are not 2D as well (they don't have to
  be overhead like Contra 3's))

- Anything else related to the creation of levels

Your first task is to talk to the Storyboard agent and Engineering in order to convert the first level of the game,
which used to be a tutorial, into a full-length playable level with numerous monsters, platforms, foreground objects to
jump on/over, etc.

Feel free to do some research into other 2D Contra (and clone) games if you are not already a level design expert. Don't
copy anything directly but feel free to learn from other game designs.

responsibilities_assumed[0]:

Level layout, platform placement, jump reachability and art/collision alignment.

responsibilities_assumed[1]:

Enemy placement, encounter pacing, finite wave budgets, safe entrances and escape lanes.

responsibilities_assumed[2]:

Level length and measured pacing; future alternative formats remain exploratory.

responsibilities_assumed[3]:

Arena and route requirements with Game Mechanics, Art, Storyboarding and Engineering.

responsibilities_assumed[4]:

Coordinated implementation of assigned geometry/integration modules and their tests; ownership must be agreed before
shared edits.

authority:

Latest explicit user instructions govern; current repository AGENTS.md and supplied successor instructions are active.
Historical predecessor claims about replacement AGENTS instructions are historical only. Runtime is evidence of
implemented behavior, not independent authorization to override user requirements.

predecessor_full_handoff:

This handoff covers everything recoverable from my current conversation context, including the earlier compacted
summary. It is historical evidence, not a fresh inspection of the relocated repository. Your current file inspection and
Engineering’s latest delivery records should establish what is actually shipped.

## Role, collaborators, and ownership (archived occurrence 2)

Sean assigned this chat Level Design: platform placement, level length and pacing, enemy placement/counts, environmental
objects players can climb, coordination with Storyboarding and Art, and eventually alternative level formats.

The immediate objective was to turn the former tutorial into a full first level with dense action, meaningful
platforming, varied enemy entrances, and the Clogfather at the end. Sean explicitly requested coordination with Game
Mechanics and asked Engineering to present the combined report.

Existing collaborators:

| Role | Chat ID |
|---|---|
| Engineering | `01a1375f-7657-7580-b16e-57a6f5925efd` |
| Game Mechanics | `01a138a7-cc43-7420-a950-55b935ddde25` |
| Bestiary / Art | `01a1388b-2b60-7011-832a-5c6a044e0028` |
| Storyboarding | `01a1387e-545b-7671-943e-3d36a1c88db7` |

Engineering owned integration, base game changes, builds, versions, roadmap, storybook, and the final user report. My
edits were assigned subsets, coordinated to avoid shared-file collisions. Mechanics owned weapon balance and later the
Widow behavior. Art owned artwork and asset manifests.

The project was relocated to `/Users/sfoley/repos/drain_damage`. Do not recreate `/Users/sfoley/repos/plumbing_inc`.

## User intent and corrections (archived occurrence 2)

The original Contra screenshots illustrated the intended feel:

- Many enemies arriving from both horizontal sides.
- Flying threats and powerups.
- Stationary elevated shooters and projectile throwers.
- Ground, ceiling, background-to-foreground, and viewer-to-play-plane entrances.
- Architecture and debris supporting platforms, rather than repeated floating slabs.
- Ordinary enemies generally dying in one to three hits.
- Sean’s weapon firing much faster than the perceived two-to-three-second interval.
- Distinct weapon identities for Tony and Sean, with potentially character-exclusive additional weapons.
- A full level culminating in the Clogfather.

Those were direction and requests for design consultation, not approval of every possible new enemy or entrance
mechanic.

Subsequent explicit corrections, relayed through Mechanics/Engineering and implemented within my assigned scope:

1. **Remove every first-level kill-count progress gate.** Replace gated encounters with larger crowds and finite waves
   along a freely traversable route.

2. **Make the flying spider twice its linear size and durable.** This became a deliberate exception to ordinary enemies’
   one-to-three-hit target.

3. **Give the Widow an actual proximity swoop attack matching its magazine description.** A passive fixed hover was
   superseded.

4. **Audit contrast across every first-level platform.** The initial pale/ivory treatment did not cover the large
   machinery. The later direction was dank moss/gray-green frames and readable treads, dark undercuts, no beige or glow.

5. **Exactly one checkpoint near halfway through the level.** This superseded three checkpoint machines. One sleeping
   clone should occupy the sole machine; after dispensing, it should be empty.

6. Preserve three lives, scenery, and free traversal.
7. During the daughter’s playtest, do not foreground or launch the game. All my checks remained headless.

## Last delivered level structure (archived occurrence 2)

The active first-level implementation was:

`integrated_first_call.gd` → `opening_trial_game.gd` → `game.gd`

Engineering switched normal Start to the integrated class. The old trial class remained as an internal
implementation/test dependency; it was no longer intended as the normal separate timed experience.

The integrated level:

- World width: **9,600 pixels**.
- Temporary completion threshold: **x = 9,450**.
- Player starting x: **85**.
- Opening replacement ends at **x = 3,120**.
- The opening uses the first three platform modules from an earlier 6,000-pixel trial layout.
- Remaining authored scenery stays in its original world coordinates.
- The opening continues beyond 20 seconds; no timed-results stop.
- Checkpoint/respawn does not recreate opening enemies or reset world progress.
- The final delivered checkpoint constant was **`CHECKPOINTS := [4650.0]`**.

### Why x4650 (archived occurrence 2)

The midpoint between spawn85 and exit9450 is4767.5. The existing cabinet rendering would overlap the next platform
beginning at4850 if placed at that exact midpoint.

At checkpoint4650:

- Progress is approximately49% of the playable route.
- The floor is clear between the previous obstacle ending4000 and the next beginning4850.
- Cabinet visible bounds were approximately4643.5–4776.4.
- This leaves approximately73.6 pixels before the next platform.
- No scenery or collision layout needed moving.

The existing integrated loops derive machine placement, activation triggers, and enemy-spawn exclusions from
`Level.CHECKPOINTS`; changing the constant removed the other placements automatically.

## Ungated encounter implementation (archived occurrence 2)

I delivered this in `scripts/integrated_first_call.gd` and updated `tests/integrated_opening.gd`.

### Opening (archived occurrence 3)

Twelve finite opening event positions:

`100, 250, 450, 600, 800, 1000, 1200, 1400, 1650, 1900, 2200, 2450`

Their eligibility times were set to zero in the integrated copy of the layout. Position determines when they become
eligible; safety and population checks can defer them.

Passing the opening marks remaining unissued opening events unavailable. It does not award kills. Existing live actors
persist until normal retirement/death.

### Later waves (archived occurrence 2)

| Range | Maximum arrivals |
|---|---:|
| x3500–4800 | 8 |
| x4900–6480 | 10 |
| x6860–8160 | 10 |
| x8400–9380 | 8 |

Total: **36 later runners**, up from the old22 encounter quota, plus the opening’s12 potential events.

Important distinctions:

- These are finite available arrivals, not a guarantee the player must fight all48 enemies.
- Moving past a wave’s range bypasses its unissued remainder.
- No kill requirement blocks forward travel or the temporary exit.
- Arrivals are staggered by at least0.65 seconds.
- At most one arrival is created per encounter update.
- Deferred arrivals do not accumulate into a burst.
- Front/rear entrance selection prefers rear every third arrival and tries the alternate side if needed.
- Entrances are offscreen.
- Player clearance is measured from the expanded visible Flushfiend bounds, not merely its anchor.
- Required clearance:220 pixels.
- Spawn placement avoids scenery, other bodies, opening actors, and checkpoint proximity.
- Checkpoint exclusion: within200 pixels of its location.
- Enemies sufficiently far behind the camera retire without kill credit.
- Retirement threshold used `enemy.pos.x + 38 < camera_x - 320`.
- No live threats are deleted merely to satisfy a population cap.

### Population budget (archived occurrence 2)

- Eight bodies normally.
- Six while a live or warning/entering Widow exists.
- One Widow at a time.
- A new Widow is deferred when population is already six.
- Population conservatively counts campaign enemies, live opening actors, and live swarm actors.

Mechanics also wanted two shooters, six hostile bullets, and three independent attack patterns. Opening state already
managed those attack budgets. The later waves I added were runners, not additional shooters.

### Gates and completion (archived occurrence 2)

All six former shutters were removed from the integrated level’s:

- Collision.
- Projectile blocking.
- Drawing.
- Kill-count progression.

Legacy mission arrays/indices remained for compatibility/telemetry:

- `mission.zone_counts.fill(0)`
- `mission.current_zone = 2`
- `gate_progress.fill(1)`

The integrated movement and projectile-solid methods use the replacement level geometry, not the legacy gate geometry.

HUD changed to following the service route rather than an enemy quota.

**The Clogfather was still missing at my last verified handoff.** Completion at9450 was explicitly temporary, and
Engineering was told not to describe the boss milestone as complete.

## Combat and Widow details (archived occurrence 2)

The working opening balance at my last checks:

| Character | Damage | Attack interval |
|---|---:|---:|
| Tony | 12 | 0.16 seconds |
| Sean | 18 | 0.24 seconds |

Later runners use24HP, giving two hits from either default weapon.

The Widow:

- Twice its original linear render size.
- Collision expanded consistently to60×48.
- 180HP.
- Fifteen Tony hits or ten Sean hits.
- Approximate first-to-last-hit time under uninterrupted fire:2.24 seconds Tony,2.16 seconds Sean.
- This deliberately superseded the general ordinary-enemy one-to-three-hit target.

### Superseded hover behavior (archived occurrence 2)

Mechanics first delivered a proximity-triggered fixed four-second hover, then resumed leftward travel. This was
explicitly replaced after Sean requested a real swoop matching the magazine.

### Latest swoop handoff received (archived occurrence 2)

Mechanics reported:

-0.65-second visible warning.

- Aim locked at warning start.

-0.8-second committed dive.

- No tracking after commitment.
- Expanded full-body sweep against `player_shot_solids`, including floor.
- Blocked dive aborts into a one-second return.

-1.2-second cooldown.
-2× scale and180HP preserved.

- Tests cover open-floor dive, warning, fixed aim, recovery, and deck nonpenetration.

I did not edit the parent adapter during that work; Mechanics owned it.

### Clearance constraints I supplied (archived occurrence 2)

Opening decks are250×16 at y320. With flyer center/base lane250 and a24-pixel shallow arc, its doubled bounds originally
stayed above the deck.

For the attack:

- Gaps between plinths and decks are only40 or50 pixels.
- A60-pixel-wide flyer cannot safely pass through them.
- The under-deck opening is104 pixels high, from336 to440.
- A48-pixel flyer plus46-pixel hero nearly fills that space.
- Do not trap the hero under a deck with the large body.
- Sweep against real solids, abort blocked descent, and preserve an escape direction.
- Only the actual play-plane body should deal contact damage.

## Other implementation details worth preserving (archived occurrence 2)

### Shared projectile update (archived occurrence 2)

The integrated class had to avoid advancing friendly bullets twice through parent/base combat:

1. Save friendly shots and temporarily give base combat an empty list while it moves campaign enemies.
2. Restore shots.
3. Run one integrated closest-hit sweep across scenery, opening actors, campaign enemies, and swarm.
4. Temporarily hide friendly shots again when calling the parent’s hostile-shot update.

Do not casually replace this with both parent updates; it previously required explicit single-travel regression
coverage.

### Flushfiend collision and death audio (archived occurrence 2)

- Visible projectile hit body is43 pixels tall.
- Helper returns `Rect2(feet + Vector2(-19,-43), Vector2(38,43))`.
- Movement/contact body remains36 pixels tall.
- Opening runner/rear-runner hit handling uses the visible projectile body.
- On lethal opening runner/rear-runner damage, call `flushfiend_death_audio.play_defeat()`.
- Do not use that audio for shooter, emerger, or flyer.
- Engineering owns base campaign Flushfiend audio hooks.

### Swarm (archived occurrence 2)

- The optional swarm at2090 was removed from the replaced opening.
- The later swarm at4400 was retained.
- Do not conflate it with the Hallowed Widow.
- Its behavior was not otherwise redesigned by my work.
- Base/swarm geometry hooks were adjusted by Engineering to respect the integrated geometry.

### Checkpoints and reset (archived occurrence 2)

`preserving_respawn` prevents respawn from resetting the opening observation/world state. Full restart resets it.

Respawn clears hostile-shot state/tokens, preserves progress and weapon, and respects normal life handling.

No pending wastewater change was assigned at my last handoff.

## Validation history and limits (archived occurrence 2)

### Ungated integration (archived occurrence 2)

`tests/integrated_opening.gd` passed for both characters, covering:

- Actual run/jump/shoot traversal beyond20 seconds.
- Seamless opening boundary.
- Friendly projectile advances exactly once.
- Former gates passable in both directions.
- Former gates absent from projectile solids.
- Crowds grow beyond four without kills.
- Global population cap.
- Widow population cap.
- No burst backlog.
- Bypass/retirement does not award kills.
- Checkpoint/respawn preserves state.
- Temporary exit independent of remaining enemies.
- Full restart restores three lives.

An early fixture failed because it parked newly spawned enemies at the entrances without advancing combat movement. The
fixture was corrected to advance bodies between arrival updates; this was a test setup issue.

An earlier exit fixture also accidentally crossed an unactivated checkpoint, causing its dispenser to move the player
before completion. The fixture was corrected to establish the intended checkpoint state.

- Respawn returned to4650 while preserving the test’s two remaining lives.
- Full reset restored three lives.
- Exit fixture used checkpoint index0.
- Actual30-second traversal reached approximately8139.7 for Tony and7098.3 for Sean.
- Test exited0.

### Diagnostics and visual gaps (archived occurrence 2)

Headless tests emitted existing diagnostics:

- Cannot write `user://logs/...`.
- macOS certificate lookup error.
- ObjectDB/resource shutdown warnings in some tests.

I reported these rather than describing output as entirely clean.

I did **not** launch a game window, perform final visual composition review, create storybook captures, or build the
final combined delivery in these handoffs. Engineering owned those steps.

Do not turn source-image inspection into a claim that the new art was visually checked in the running game.

## Exact status after x4650 handoff (archived occurrence 2)

**No unresolved implementation task remained assigned to this Level Design chat.** I released the files to Engineering
after tests passed.

Outstanding dependencies and product work remained:

1. Engineering’s single sleeping clone and spent/empty machine behavior.
2. Engineering’s base-game/dispenser regression updates.
3. Combined build and user-facing delivery.
4. Running-game visual review of new platform art.
5. Appropriate roadmap/storybook reconciliation.
6. Clogfather boss encounter and real end-of-level completion.
7. Broader requested enemy diversity, entry styles, powerups, and a fully authored action cadence—not all were
   implemented by simply adding runner waves.

The checkpoint delivery was intended for Engineering’s next build, discussed as build51. That was an integration target,
**not my confirmation that build51 shipped**. Likewise earlier platform work targeted combined49; verify actual build
records.

### Dependent tests explicitly flagged to Engineering (archived occurrence 2)

- `tests/employee_dispenser.gd`: old6660/8190 coordinates and checkpoint indices1/2.
- `tests/basement.gd`: hardcoded `range(3)` and “three checkpoints” wording.
- `tests/dispenser_registration.gd`:3300 appeared as an arbitrary geometry sample, not necessarily a real checkpoint
  requirement.

Engineering owned those files. I did not silently update them.

## Reconciling stale gates, three checkpoints, and timed-trial records (archived occurrence 2)

Use this authority order:

1. Sean’s latest direct instructions and corrections.
2. Current integrated runtime and its passing tests.
3. Engineering’s latest verified build/delivery records.
4. Current design documents updated to match those.
5. Historical documents and old implementation classes as historical context.

Specific intended reconciliation:

- **Gates:** no kill-count gates in the integrated first level. Legacy `GATES`, mission classes, gate tests, and base
  implementation may still exist. Their existence does not make them current integrated gameplay.

- **Checkpoints:** one at4650 supersedes3300/6660/8190.
- **Timed trial:** continuous normal first-level opening supersedes the separate20-second experiment. Trial filenames
  and parent classes remain implementation history, not proof of a timed mode in normal Start.

- **Platform palette:** moss-green v3 supersedes ivory v2 for all20 raised surfaces.
- **Widow behavior:** committed swoop supersedes passive four-second hover.
- **Boss:** temporary x9450 exit must remain labeled incomplete until the Clogfather actually exists and controls
  completion.

Preserve historical storybook iterations and source artwork. Update current roadmap/design claims without rewriting
history as though earlier iterations never existed.

Engineering was explicitly asked to reconcile roadmap/storybook with each delivery. I did not verify that all those
updates happened. Your observation that current records still describe obsolete behavior is a real reconciliation task,
not evidence that Sean reversed these corrections.

## Instructions and provenance caveat (archived occurrence 2)

Earlier project instructions included Art ownership, playtest permission, build notifications, roadmap/storybook
maintenance, character naming, and bestiary/combat metadata rules.

Later Sean supplied an AGENTS.md replacement saying it **replaces all previously provided AGENTS.md instructions**,
retaining global coding conventions. Therefore do not mechanically treat the earlier pasted project instructions as
still authoritative solely because they appear in this history. Read the current repository guidance and honor direct
user instructions and active coordination commitments.

The explicit daughter-playtest restriction and assigned file ownership were separate concrete coordination instructions
during those tasks.

## Access diagnostic (archived occurrence 2)

On October17, my existing chat attempted normal-permission temporary-file creation directly in the renamed root.

Result:

- Working directory: `/Users/sfoley/repos/drain_damage`.
- Creation failed: `PermissionError(1, 'Operation not permitted')`.
- No file was created; cleanup was unnecessary.
- No escalation, workaround, project modification, or permission change.
- This chat’s configured writable project root still pointed to the obsolete `/Users/sfoley/repos/plumbing_inc`.

A new chat’s successful write test does not retroactively establish that this predecessor chat gained access. Your
successor environment should use its own verified permissions.

## Lost context and unsaved requests (archived occurrence 2)

The conversation was compacted before the ungating work resumed. I have the detailed compacted summary plus subsequent
visible turns, but not verbatim access in this response to every pre-compaction tool result or discussion.

Consequently:

- I cannot honestly certify that every original design rationale or mockup detail survived compaction.
- I know of no unsaved user request or unfinished Level Design-owned edit after the4650 handoff.
- The major unresolved user objective is still the complete first level, especially the Clogfather and broader
  enemy/encounter variety.

- Your recovery of all17 available turns and inspection of repository handoffs is the best complement to this account.
- Check original `design/first-call-action/`, `handoffs/`, roadmap, and storybook records for earlier mockups and
  narrative decisions; do not invent missing decisions from filenames.

> const SEAN_HEIGHT := 72.0
> # Generated poses cross uniform cell boundaries. Explicit search areas exclude neighboring limbs.
> const SEAN_AREAS: Array[Rect2i] = [
>         Rect2i(0, 150, 310, 460), Rect2i(312, 150, 312, 460),
>         Rect2i(640, 150, 304, 460), Rect2i(945, 150, 309, 460),
>         Rect2i(0, 730, 310, 430), Rect2i(340, 680, 277, 360),
>         Rect2i(615, 740, 340, 420), Rect2i(956, 740, 298, 420),
> ]
> var sean_frames: Array[Rect2] = []
> var tony_frames: Array[Rect2] = []
> var toilet_frames: Array[Rect2] = []
>
> func _init() -> void:
>         var image := SEAN.get_image()
>         for area in SEAN_AREAS:
>                 sean_frames.append(opaque_region(image, area))
>         tony_frames = atlas_regions(TONY, 4, 2)
>         toilet_frames = atlas_regions(TOILET, 2, 2)
>
> func atlas_regions(texture: Texture2D, columns: int, rows: int) -> Array[Rect2]:
>         var result: Array[Rect2] = []
>         var image := texture.get_image()
>         var cell := Vector2i(image.get_width() / columns, image.get_height() / rows)
>         for row in range(rows):
>                 for column in range(columns):
>                         var origin := Vector2i(column, row) * cell
>                         result.append(opaque_region(image, Rect2i(origin, cell)))
>         return result
>
> func opaque_region(image: Image, area: Rect2i) -> Rect2:
>         # Ignore nearly transparent generation fringes when aligning frames to their feet.
>         var minimum := area.end
>         var maximum := area.position
>         for y in range(area.position.y, area.end.y):
>                 for x in range(area.position.x, area.end.x):
>                         if image.get_pixel(x, y).a > 0.2:
>                                 minimum = minimum.min(Vector2i(x, y))
>                                 maximum = maximum.max(Vector2i(x + 1, y + 1))
>         return Rect2(minimum, maximum - minimum)
>
> func sprite(game: Node2D, texture: Texture2D, region: Rect2, feet: Vector2,
>                 scale: float, flip: bool, tint := Color.WHITE) -> void:
>         var size := region.size * scale
>         var destination := Rect2(Vector2(floorf(feet.x - size.x / 2), floorf(feet.y - size.y)), size)
>         # Negative destination width mirrors the atlas region without changing its foot anchor.
>         if flip:
>                 destination.size.x = -destination.size.x
>         game.draw_texture_rect_region(texture, destination, region, tint)
>
> func draw_sean(game: Node2D) -> void:
>         var frame := 0
>         if not game.grounded:

>         elif absf(game.velocity.x) > 20:

>         if game.fire_timer > game.weapon.attack_interval_seconds - 0.10:

>         if game.invulnerable > 1.15:

>         var texture: Texture2D = TONY if game.employee.id == "tony" else SEAN

> #!/usr/bin/env python3
> """Validate the authored first-call brief and prepare an integration plan without changing runtime."""
>
> import json
> from pathlib import Path
> from typing import Any
>
> ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
> SOURCE = ROOT / "levels/first-call/level-design.json"
> OUTPUT = ROOT / "handoffs/first-call-runtime-plan.json"
> ENEMY_WIDTH = 38
> SPAWN_MARGIN = 32
>
>
> def clear_spawn(x: int, terrain: list[list[int]], checkpoints: list[int], radius: int) -> bool:
>     """Check a floor spawn against solid scenery and checkpoint recovery footprints."""
>     if any(x < left + width + SPAWN_MARGIN and x + ENEMY_WIDTH > left - SPAWN_MARGIN
>            for left, _, width, _ in terrain):
>         return False
>     return not any(x < point + radius and x + ENEMY_WIDTH > point - radius for point in checkpoints)
>
>
> def prepare(source: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
>     """Resolve legal floor homes and report constraints requiring explicit design decisions."""
>     world = source["world"]
>     checkpoints = source["recovery"]["checkpoint_x"]
>     radius = source["recovery"]["clear_radius_pixels"]
>     terrain = [rect for zone in source["zones"] for rect in zone["terrain"]]
>     for x, y, width, height in terrain:
>         if width < 120 or height <= 0 or y + height != world["floor_y"]:
>             raise ValueError(f"Invalid grounded terrain: {(x, y, width, height)}")
>         if x < 0 or x + width > world["width"]:
>             raise ValueError("Terrain outside world")
>     zones = []
>     total = 0
>     previous_end = 0
>     for zone in source["zones"]:
>         start, end = zone["x"]
>         if start != previous_end or end <= start:
>             raise ValueError("Zone bounds must form a contiguous ordered route")
>         previous_end = end
>         homes: list[int] = []
>         for requested in zone["enemy_x"]:
>             candidates = sorted(range(start + SPAWN_MARGIN, end - ENEMY_WIDTH - SPAWN_MARGIN),
>                                 key=lambda value: (abs(value - requested), value))
>             home = next((x for x in candidates if clear_spawn(x, terrain, checkpoints, radius)
>                          and all(abs(x - prior) >= ENEMY_WIDTH + SPAWN_MARGIN for prior in homes)), None)
>             if home is None:
>                 raise ValueError(f"No legal spawn pocket for {zone['id']} at {requested}")
>             homes.append(home)
>         total += len(homes)
>         zones.append({"id": zone["id"], "bounds": [start, end], "authored_homes": zone["enemy_x"],
>                       "clear_floor_homes": homes, "terrain": zone["terrain"]})
>     if total != source["encounter_contract"]["total_authored_enemies"] or previous_end != world["width"]:
>         raise ValueError("Enemy total or final zone extent mismatch")
>     return {
>         "status": "Prepared integration plan, not runtime data; awaiting art and encounter-gating decision",
>         "source": str(SOURCE.relative_to(ROOT)), "world": world, "zones": zones, "enemy_total": total,
>         "checkpoints": checkpoints, "active_cap": source["encounter_contract"]["maximum_active_attackers"],
>         "spawn_note": "Homes are clear of solids and recovery footprints. Actual activation must also check live
> player, camera, landing zones and active-enemy overlap.",
>         "blocking_decisions": [
>             "Prop textures and art-aligned collision metadata are requested from Bestiary / Art; no substitute scenery
> permitted.",
>             "Finite queued encounters, four active enemies and no behind-player spawns require progression gates or a
> revised activation rule. A player can otherwise rush beyond all legal ahead-spawn pockets.",
>             "Recommended: visible encounter gates hold progress before the final safe spawn pocket, open only after
> that zone's finite queue clears, and preserve surviving pursuit on death. Gate art and explicit Level Design alignment
> are required.",
>             "120/180px grounded props require staircase-aware enemy jumps, not vertical snapping or walking through
> them. Test approaches from both sides and every gap."
>         ],
>         "unchanged": "Prototype 26 remains the runnable/exported game until the complete integration is validated."
>     }
>
>
> if __name__ == "__main__":
>     result = prepare(json.loads(SOURCE.read_text()))
>     OUTPUT.write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2) + "\n")

>                 if rect.position.y < 0 or rect.end.y > FLOOR_Y: return false
>                 match item.get("collision", ""):
>                         "solid": next_plinths.append(rect)
>                         "one_way_top": next_decks.append(rect)
>                         _: return false
>         var rules: Dictionary = layout.get("placement_rules", {})
>         var grates: Dictionary = rules.get("visible_grate", {})
>         for x in grates.get("candidate_x", []):
>                 var value := float(x)
>                 if not is_finite(value) or value < 0 or value > WIDTH: return false
>                 next_grates.append(value)
>         plinths = next_plinths
>         decks = next_decks
>         grate_x = next_grates
>         return true
>
> func projectile_solids() -> Array[Rect2]:
>         ## Both faces of a deck block shots; one-way behavior applies only to moving bodies.
>         var result: Array[Rect2] = plinths.duplicate()
>         result.append_array(decks)
>         return result
>
> func move_body(position: Vector2, velocity: Vector2, size: Vector2, delta: float) -> Dictionary:
>         ## Return pos, velocity and grounded. Input position must not begin embedded in solid scenery.
>         var pos := position
>         var vel := velocity
>         var grounded := false
>         # Bound displacement per sweep so diagonal movement cannot skip a thin deck corner.
>         var steps := maxi(1, int(ceil(velocity.length() * maxf(delta, 0.0) / MAX_STEP)))
>         var dt := maxf(delta, 0.0) / steps
>         for step in range(steps):
>                 var old := pos
>                 var target_x := pos.x + vel.x * dt
>                 for rect in plinths:
>                         if old.y >= rect.end.y - EPSILON or old.y + size.y <= rect.position.y + EPSILON:
>                                 continue
>                         if vel.x > 0 and old.x + size.x <= rect.position.x + EPSILON:
>                                 if target_x + size.x > rect.position.x: target_x = minf(target_x, rect.position.x -
> size.x)
>                         elif vel.x < 0 and old.x >= rect.end.x - EPSILON:
>                                 if target_x < rect.end.x: target_x = maxf(target_x, rect.end.x)
>                 pos.x = clampf(target_x, 22.0, width - 20.0 - size.x)
>                 if not is_equal_approx(pos.x, old.x + vel.x * dt): vel.x = 0
>                 var target_y := pos.y + vel.y * dt
>                 grounded = false
>                 for rect in projectile_solids():
>                         if pos.x + size.x <= rect.position.x or pos.x >= rect.end.x: continue
>                         if vel.y >= 0 and old.y + size.y <= rect.position.y + EPSILON:
>                                 if target_y + size.y >= rect.position.y:
>                                         target_y = minf(target_y, rect.position.y - size.y)
>                                         grounded = true
>                         elif vel.y < 0 and rect in plinths and old.y >= rect.end.y - EPSILON:
>                                 if target_y < rect.end.y: target_y = maxf(target_y, rect.end.y)
>                 if target_y + size.y >= floor_y:
>                         target_y = floor_y - size.y
>                         grounded = true
>                 if grounded or not is_equal_approx(target_y, old.y + vel.y * dt): vel.y = 0
>                 pos.y = target_y
>         return {"pos": pos, "velocity": vel, "grounded": grounded}
>
> func visible_deck(camera: float, player: Vector2, size: Vector2) -> Dictionary:
>         ## size is the proposed enemy collider, player is hero top-left. Empty means defer.
>         var best: Dictionary = {}
>         for rect in decks:
>                 if rect.position.x < camera or rect.end.x > camera + VIEW_WIDTH or size.x > rect.size.x: continue
>                 var feet := Vector2(rect.end.x - maxf(28.0, size.x / 2.0), rect.position.y)
>                 var gap := feet.x - player.x
>                 if gap < 220 or gap > 420: continue
>                 var pos := feet - Vector2(size.x / 2.0, size.y)
>                 if best.is_empty() or pos.x < best.pos.x:
>                         best = {"pos": pos, "feet": feet, "surface": rect}
>         return best
>
> func visible_grate(camera: float, player: Vector2, size: Vector2) -> Dictionary:
>         ## Caller additionally checks predicted landing, live actors and warning-time safety.
>         for x in grate_x:
>                 var pos := Vector2(x - size.x / 2.0, floor_y - size.y)
>                 if pos.x < camera or pos.x + size.x > camera + VIEW_WIDTH: continue
>                 if x - player.x < 180 or x - player.x > 420: continue
>                 var blocked := false
>                 for rect in projectile_solids():
>                         if Rect2(pos, size).intersects(rect): blocked = true
>                 if not blocked: return {"pos": pos, "feet": Vector2(x, floor_y)}
>         return {}

>         var art: Dictionary = data.get(\"art\", {})
>         var sections: Array = data.get(\"progression\", [])
>         if sections.is_empty(): return false
>         for entry: Dictionary in art.get(\"assets\", []):
>                 var path: String = \"res://\" + str(entry.path).trim_prefix(\"res://\")
>                 if not textures.has(path):
>                         var texture = load(path)
>                         if not texture is Texture2D: return false
>                         textures[path] = texture
>                 var values: Array = entry.source_rect
>                 var source := Rect2(values[0], values[1], values[2], values[3])
>                 var texture: Texture2D = textures[path]
>                 if source.size.x <= 0 or source.size.y <= 0: return false
>                 if not Rect2(Vector2.ZERO, texture.get_size()).encloses(source): return false
>                 assets[entry.id] = {\"path\":path, \"source\":source,
>                         \"size\":Vector2(entry.destination_size[0], entry.destination_size[1]),
>                         \"anchor\":Vector2(entry.anchor[0], entry.anchor[1])}
>                 if entry.get(\"role\") == \"floating-debris\": debris_ids.append(str(entry.id))
>         for id in [\"interior\", \"rim-top\", \"rim-bottom\", \"water-a\", \"grease-light\",
>                         \"grease-medium\", \"grease-heavy\", \"plug\"]:
>                 if not assets.has(id): return false
>         if debris_ids.is_empty(): return false
>         profile.configure(sections)
>         ready = true
>         return true
>
> func draw(game: Node2D, camera: float, clock: float, trial := false) -> void:
>         ## Call inside _draw with world transform active. Does not change transforms or advance time.
>         for command: Dictionary in commands(camera, clock, trial):
>                 var destination: Rect2 = command.rect
>                 if command.flip: destination.size.x = -destination.size.x
>                 game.draw_texture_rect_region(textures[assets[command.id].path], destination,
>                         command.source, Color(1, 1, 1, command.alpha))
>
> func commands(camera: float, clock: float, trial := false) -> Array[Dictionary]:
>         ## Deterministic, inspectable draw plan. Frozen gameplay clock means frozen animation.
>         var output: Array[Dictionary] = []
>         if not ready: return output
>         var limit := 6000.0 if trial else 9600.0
>         var left := maxf(0, floorf(camera / STRIP) * STRIP)
>         var right := minf(limit, camera + 960)
>         var x := left
>         while x < right:
>                 var end := minf(x + STRIP, right)
>                 if not trial and x < profile.terminal_x and end > profile.terminal_x:
>                         end = profile.terminal_x
>                 var state: Dictionary = profile.sample((x + end) / 2.0, trial)
>                 _tiled(output, \"interior\", x, end, 464, 56, 0)
>                 if state.water > 0:
>                         _tiled(output, \"water-a\", x, end, 520 - 56 * state.water, 56 * state.water,
>                                 maxf(clock, 0) * state.flow)
>                 x = end
>         _add_debris(output, left, right, maxf(clock, 0), trial)
>         x = left
>         while x < right:
>                 var end := minf(x + STRIP, right)
>                 if not trial and x < profile.terminal_x and end > profile.terminal_x:
>                         end = profile.terminal_x
>                 var state: Dictionary = profile.sample((x + end) / 2.0, trial)
>                 if state.coverage >= 1:
>                         _tiled(output, \"plug\", x, end, 464, 56, 0)
>                 else:
>                         # Crossfade source art without a visible tier switch; both share the sampled height.
>                         var scaled: float = clampf(state.coverage * 2, 0, 2)
>                         var ids := [\"grease-light\", \"grease-medium\", \"grease-heavy\"]
>                         var index := mini(int(scaled), 1)
>                         var blend := scaled - index
>                         _tiled(output, ids[index], x, end, 520 - 56 * state.coverage, 56 * state.coverage, 0)
>                         _tiled(output, ids[index + 1], x, end, 520 - 56 * state.coverage,
>                                 56 * state.coverage, 0, blend)
>                 _tiled(output, \"rim-top\", x, end, 456, 8, 0)
>                 _tiled(output, \"rim-bottom\", x, end, 520, 8, 0)
>                 x = end
>         return output
>
> func _tiled(output: Array[Dictionary], id: String, left: float, right: float,
>                 y: float, height: float, phase: float, alpha := 1.0) -> void:
>         if height <= 0 or alpha <= 0: return
>         var x := left
>         while x < right:
>                 var tile := int(floor((x - phase) / TILE))
>                 var local := fposmod(x - phase, TILE)
>                 var span := minf(right - x, TILE - local)
>                 if span < 0.00001: break
>                 var mirrored := posmod(tile, 2) == 1
>                 var source: Rect2 = assets[id].source
>                 var unit: float = source.size.x / TILE
>                 source.position.x += (TILE - local - span if mirrored else local) * unit
>                 source.size.x = span * unit
>                 output.append({\"id\":id, \"rect\":Rect2(x, y, span, height), \"source\":source,
>                         \"flip\":mirrored, \"alpha\":alpha})
>                 x += span
>
> func _add_debris(output: Array[Dictionary], left: float, right: float, clock: float, trial: bool) -> void:
>         # Stable world cells, not camera-relative RNG. Fade at wrap boundaries prevents popping.
>         for cell in range(maxi(0, int(floor(left / 240))), int(ceil(right / 240))):
>                 var origin := float(cell * 240)
>                 var state: Dictionary = profile.sample(origin + 120, trial)
>                 if state.water < 0.08 or state.flow <= 0: continue
>                 var local := fposmod(float(posmod(cell * 97 + 53, 240)) + clock * state.flow, 240)
>                 var x := origin + local
>                 var at: Dictionary = profile.sample(x, trial)
>                 if at.water < 0.08 or at.flow <= 0: continue
>                 var alpha: float = minf(local / 24, (240 - local) / 24) * (1 - at.coverage)
>                 alpha = clampf(alpha, 0, 0.85)
>                 var id: String = debris_ids[posmod(cell * 5 + 1, debris_ids.size())]
>                 var asset: Dictionary = assets[id]
>                 var rect := Rect2(Vector2(x, 520 - 56 * at.water + 3) - asset.anchor, asset.size)
>                 var clip := rect.intersection(Rect2(left, 464, right - left, 56))
>                 if not clip.has_area(): continue
>                 var source: Rect2 = asset.source
>                 var ratio := source.size / rect.size
>                 source.position += (clip.position - rect.position) * ratio
>                 source.size = clip.size * ratio
>                 output.append({\"id\":id, \"rect\":clip, \"source\":source, \"flip\":false, \"alpha\":alpha})

> # Global coding standards
>
> Read the sections relevant to the task before editing or reviewing that file type. These defaults supplement
> `AGENTS.md`; explicit user requests and applicable project conventions take precedence. Tool-specific guidance
> applies when that tool is used, not as a requirement to add it to every project.
>
> ## General engineering and file hygiene
>

> - Read existing code and configuration first. Reuse established helpers and command entry points before adding tools.

> - Keep changes scoped to the requested behavior. Avoid speculative abstractions, unrelated cleanup, and comments or
>   annotations added to untouched code. Comment why a non-obvious decision exists rather than restating the code.

> - Keep utilities cohesive, generic, and dependency-light. Preserve established package/import boundaries. Add public
>   re-exports only intentionally; internal helpers need not become public APIs.

> - Use named constants or enums for meaningful identifiers, opcodes, and status values instead of unexplained literals.

> - Text files have no trailing whitespace, truly empty blank lines, and exactly one final newline. Preserve required
>   format semantics and existing encoding/line endings. New text defaults to UTF-8.

> - Default to a 120-character maximum for Python, Markdown, and shell, including comments and banners. Wrap logically,
>   without routinely wrapping at 80. Do not split a literal or machine-readable value in a way that changes its
> meaning.

> - Follow existing language/format settings for other files. Preserve user changes and keep temporary artifacts in an
>   ignored scratch location, preferably the project's existing one. Do not add scratch output to source trees or
> commits.

> - Read complete relevant tool output. Recover from errors or truncation with a focused read, structured output, or a
>   suitable retry before drawing conclusions. Report unresolved gaps; do not present partial results as complete.

> - Use fictional, neutral examples for reusable code and documentation. Keep proprietary implementations, private
>   names,
>   internal URLs, credentials, and business-specific behavior out of cross-project material.
>
> ## Python
>
> ### Formatting, typing, and imports
>

> - Use four-space code indentation and a **120-character** limit. Keep formatter/linter widths consistent when setting
>   up
>   a new project; use an existing project's declared settings when different.

> - Type function parameters, return values, methods, and class attributes. Aim for strict type-checking quality; avoid
>   bypassing checks with unexplained `Any`, ignores, or casts. Honor deliberate project exemptions such as test
> fixtures.

> - Prefer `list[str]`, `dict[str, int]`, and `X | None` when supported by the project's Python version. Use postponed
>   annotations when the existing code or version compatibility calls for them.

> - Group imports standard library, third-party, then local, with blank lines between groups. Use the configured
>   Ruff/isort rules rather than manually fighting the sorter.

> - Preserve the supported Python version and package layout. Use the existing environment and lockfile; when the
>   project
>   uses Poetry, run tools through that environment. Ship typing metadata when maintaining a typed library that uses it.
>
> ### Docstrings and command-line tools
>

> - Document public APIs and complex behavior. Match the package's established convention: Google-style sections where
>   configured, or reST/Sphinx fields such as `:param:` and `:return:` where established. Do not mix or convert styles
>   opportunistically. For a new Python project without a preference, use Google-style docstrings consistently.

> - Keep indentation inside docstrings in multiples of two spaces; prefer four-space list continuations over three-space
>   hanging alignment. Explain important exceptions, side effects, and constraints without duplicating obvious types.

> - Prefer Click for new CLIs when already available or an appropriate dependency choice. Preserve existing CLI
>   frameworks
>   and avoid adding a dependency for a trivial script. With Click, use decorators, `click.echo()`, option `envvar`
>   support,
>   and `context_settings={"max_content_width": 120}`. Keep help informative and consistent with actual behavior.
>
> ### Async, models, and generated artifacts
>

> - In an async codebase, keep I/O asynchronous and avoid blocking the event loop. Keep sync/async bridges at deliberate
>   boundaries; do not scatter `asyncio.run()` inside library code.

> - When Pydantic v2 is already the model layer, prefer immutable models and `extra="forbid"` where those semantics fit.
>   Preserve the existing source of truth; regenerate and verify dependent schemas when models change.

> - Update generators/templates instead of hand-editing derived output. Preserve established dependency boundaries
>   between
>   authoring tools, runtime libraries, and generated artifacts; verify output remains self-contained as required.
>
> ### Linting and tests
>

> - Run the project's configured lint/format/type checks. Ruff, Ruff format, Flake8, Pylint, and mypy are conditional on
>   the
>   project; they are not a mandatory combined stack. Prefer strict mypy standards where appropriate/configured.

> - Run independent checks as separate commands so each result is visible; do not chain Ruff and mypy with `&&` in an
>   interactive tool call. Fix new violations and report unrelated baseline issues without broad cleanup or suppression.

> - In pytest projects, use focused `test_*.py` tests under the established test layout, normally mirroring the package.
>   Test meaningful behavior and regressions, isolating network, subprocess, and host-state dependencies when practical.

> - Use the configured pytest-asyncio mode for async tests. Keep hardware, container, or external-service integration
>   tests
>   separate from the default offline suite and run them only when the task and environment authorize them.

> - Run relevant tests after library changes and the repository's required completion gate. Distinguish passed checks,
>   existing failures, skipped tests, and unavailable environments. Do not claim checks ran when they did not.
>
> ## Makefiles
>

> - For new project Makefiles, make `help` the first/default target with `.DEFAULT_GOAL := help`. Included `.mk`
>   fragments
>   should cooperate with the owning Makefile rather than overriding its default goal.

> - Put an inline `## Description` on each user-facing target. Omit help comments for private helper targets. Use
>   lowercase
>   kebab-case names, `.PHONY` for non-file targets, and actual tabs for recipes.

> - Group visible targets with `##@ Section Name`. Even a short Makefile can use one `##@ Commands` group. Keep the list
>   in
>   source order with a single awk pass over `$(MAKEFILE_LIST)`; never sort the help output.

> - Use a bold banner, bold orange section headers (`\033[1;38;5;208m`), bright white target names (`\033[97m`), and
>   reset
>   (`\033[0m`). Use `printf`, not `echo`, for ANSI output. Green/red/yellow can indicate success/error/warning.

> - Make target columns wide enough for the names. When adapting an existing Makefile, preserve target behavior and its
>   default build contract unless changing them is part of the request; a narrow edit is not a mandate for a full
> rewrite.
